### OW-08

**Antimatter + annihilation.** Observe a particle and its conjugate convert into radiation. Resolve the two- and three-photon positronium channels as follow-up measurements.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L110](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L110)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [Opposite phase walls leave correlated material modes that deliver energy to two receivers](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_08_annihilation/daughter_modes/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. Observers prepare opposite phase walls on a closed four-dimer ring. During free registered evolution with fixed number guards the walls can disappear. On three-dimer wings 8,245,757 of 268,435,456 launches gave an ordered ring and excitation on both sides, against 914 on the ordered sham. After the source measurement the local energy rose at two radii on each side. The regional current has zero matrix elements within the wall-free wing subspace. These records identify annihilation of opposite order defects and energy transfer in neutral material. An electric particle–antiparticle assignment and outgoing radiation quanta remain open. A separate registered coincidence service erases an excess and a vacancy, whose free numbers are individually conserved. Before a source measurement the 65-unit wall surplus left 62.9782 units inside the ring and 1.1321 in the two guide interiors at exposure 32. On initially uniform unguarded receivers, restoring the ring consumes all four vacancies and doubles and excludes positive receiver energy. Remote material counts verify both receivers are active. A single final contact readout locates two material mismatches outside the ordered ring on 2,061,173 of 2,065,048 ordered-ring wall launches at exposure 32. On 646,526 of these launches each far guide contact carries one 32.5-unit mismatch. The same joint outcome occurred 72 times on the ordered sham and zero times with disconnected wings, each from 67,108,864 launches. The zero-contrast arm also produces distal mismatches, on 316,129 of 22,089,847 ordered-ring wall launches. On the same wall source, a calibrated far-dimer swap resolves three downward transitions near 32 exchange units. At exposure 32 four of nine frequency pairs give a resolved excess of double detections above the ordered sham. The strongest frequency pair gives 0.0010251 excess probability per launch. Cutting the guides or removing contrast eliminates a resolved excess. These readings identify energy in material transitions under a supplied timing and readout apparatus. OpenWave’s accepted liquid-crystal example leaves the outgoing radiation type open. With a fixed ring boundary, the native three-dimer guide has a single material branch and three finite normal frequencies. In 2,064,278 of 2,066,989 ordered-ring outcomes at exposure 32, one excitation reaches each wing out of 67,108,864 total wall launches. A local phase read after the ordered-ring and two-mode filters gives a simultaneous 99% lower bound of 1.9574 on the two correlations against a separable limit of one. Two supplied passive detectors both take up energy on 36,025 wall launches and seven sham launches per 67,108,864 starts. The energy accounting includes the ring read and hold. The guide has a native gap of 31.5 exchange units. Identifying the incoming walls as physical conjugate particles and demonstrating emission of radiation remain open. ### OW-12

**Quarks.** Recover fractional electric charge from a partial winding of a quark string.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L114](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L114)

- **Interval Echo · Partial.** Two polar probes covering the ten-port belt measure its integral total on moving period-three walls and same-load compact preparations. Subtracting the separately measured one-in-three bulk density gives identical fractional excesses on these controls. A stationary probe in an adjacent cell records a phase when material can enter the intervening gateway. With the registered open-boundary reference, changing the probe sign or using a matched compact preparation changes that phase outside a common fractional-charge calibration. Electric charge on the winding defect requires a field with an independent electric standard. A separate prepared written-head string has a fractional static signed-field response calibrated against an ordinary unit. Free relaxation of that polarization and the unresolved physical electric standard limit the identification. On a prepared ternary string, a differential signed contact probe recovers the same fractional material response after adding zero through four neutral pairs. All 7,590 ternary words at total 13 or 14 on the ten-port guide have simple spectrum within each translation momentum, including across histograms. On parked three-wall preparations a remote six-dimer material guide gives −0.3307 and +0.6656 ordinary units seven or eight native seams from an end belt. This is a segmented material-polarization measurement. The unit-calibrated unsplit electrode gives almost zero for two simultaneous units. The exact local port and memory symmetry algebra of the free seam and the echo is abelian. The complete uniform written-head cut gives zero imaginary response at a half turn on any state, while the two smooth wall apertures resolve opposite signed responses. A time-ordered circuit of native contacts sustains period-three material order on a twelve-port cell. Translating an equal-count compact control also gives the third-turn polarization shift. A common external material reference on the released 15-port ring and reservoir gives signed +⅔ and −⅓ readings on the same stationary states as the triple detections. A diagonal change of head phase removes every flat material twist of the unit-memory graph. The charge interpretation still depends on the smoothed periodic reference. A calibrated physical cut meter measures odd transport on return to the same four-load wall word in 630,665 of 2^30 original launches at time 16. A change of boundary phase splits the isolated dressed band by at least 0.00856873 native exchange units. Both observations rule out exact transport by the proposed rigid two-unit pump. They measure ordinary material transport; an independent electric read of this wall remains owed. This bound allows every configuration with the same ordinary load count. An upper-band composite or a different dressed-wall read requires separate evidence. With the exterior shuttered a sharp triangular face and finite energy filter at 100.448 give a persistent singlet component in three locally decoded colour triplets. At 32 free exchange units, 32,525 of 72,008 accepted face-and-three-triplet reads are antisymmetric singlet (524,288 total launches), above the one-third biseparable bound. A separate common half-turn gives a resolved dark-port signal on three-triplet records. The material loads are integral, and the fitted colour readers are supplied. The joined uppermost state gives simultaneous fractional wall records with probability at least 73.59% (neutral) or 70.55% (positive). Its energy exceeds the upper edge with a bridge deleted. Retaining the local cost of a hop into an empty site at each cut end puts a separated pair-plus-single trial above the entire joined spectrum in both cases. On the fully opened 80-cell board any stationary state confined to the three complete source owners has at most 21.10% or 24.70% joint wall probability in the neutral or positive sector. A separate threefold compact orbital on 128 owners supports an exact antisymmetric orbital singlet with zero energy gained relative to its separated fragments. With arbitrary tails and all wall charts and positions admitted, the lower-energy band below separated cluster trials yields at most 0.274% neutral or 0.226% positive three-wall reads on all three boards. In the separate three-ordinary-load sector, an open-board trimer band has at least 95.01% face probability and lies at least 26.5 native units below separated pair-plus-single configurations. Its positive ground state gives zero geometric phase under adiabatic scalar rotations. A separate fifteen-site mobile string coupled to a held commensurate reference gives at most 31.81% or 50.68% for three charged gap records in their shortest arc. Requiring three separate sets of material sites leaves only the widest arc, with at most 19.12% or 22.78% probability. On a complete 20-cell board with an outward prepared field the held 15-port extension yields 85.25% neutral and 20.27% positive tight triples on separate stationary lines. Opening a neighbouring reservoir gives 80.90% and 9.45% in the designated channels of one Hamiltonian. All 885,726 mobile-background sharp triples at lengths 12, 15 and 18 have jointly injective transfer to the complement within each sector. Binding against every fragmentation channel and physical constituent identifications remain open. Opening the federation around the same finite-filtered three-load source leaves at least 97.6% probability of a connected triple at every free time on 20, 80 and 320 cells, conditional on the 19.95% source admission. A separate colour read at one exchange unit, with no face or connectivity selection, gives a singlet yield above 27.48% per admitted source on each board. Removing contrast during flight puts this yield below 16.10%. Both laws retain positive three-triplet entanglement witnesses at that appointment. The calibrated triplet read and integer material constituents leave the physical quark identification unresolved. An energy filter on one sharp three-load face selects the simple alternating arm level at 100.8894585 native units with 8.3645 percent original-start acceptance. The admitted three orbital triplets on that guarded level form a determinant. Common and single-reader rotations gave respectively zero dark reads in 36,525 admissions and 15,517 in 36,871. On 20, 80 and 320 fully opened boards the connected-triple probability exceeds 98.34 percent at every time after source admission. At one exchange time, singlet fidelity on the rarer triplet admissions exceeds 99.92 percent on all three boards. On the 20-cell board at least 34,742 of 2^30 original starts give a certified singlet read. The native source has a spatial arm permutation and a material orbital encoding. Physical identification requires independent constituent quark charge and colour measurements. One calibrated period of the separate native material-number phase gave 36,326, 36,858 and 36,717 bright constituent reads, with zero dark. A two-thirds or negative one-third coupling would give 75 percent dark at that period. The distinct orbital colour-centre rotation gives that fraction on one reader. After a common orbital rotation on three protected receivers, four local position bases give a simultaneous 99% lower singlet-fidelity bound of 0.9997491 in 585,045 admissions from 17,179,869,184 original launches. Different rotation order and dephasing give upper bounds of 0.897471 and 0.508764. One-load calibrations predict the conjugate-pair records. An attachment to physical quarks with fractional electric charges and to the proton and neutron remains open. Four local orbital position bases after one unit of all-contact flight certify a joint connected-singlet fraction above 0.99561, 0.99776 and 0.99774 among admissions on 20, 80 and 320 cells. Changing the aperture can give high orbital fidelity and zero joint connected-singlet weight. The retained yield is 32 to 37 per million original launches at the main aperture. Independent fractional electric charges and the two nucleon identities remain to be measured. ### OW-19

**Dark matter candidate.** Demonstrate a stable massive excitation with measured neutrality as a dark-matter candidate.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L121](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/753232cac4976ab2ca208eb99d73fe7ac88ef333/MODELS.md#L121) · [MODELS.md L121](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/f8fd02b2e0d96cfa0d868259daec2c9822375d34/MODELS.md#L121) · [MODELS.md L454](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/f8fd02b2e0d96cfa0d868259daec2c9822375d34/MODELS.md#L454)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [A remote owner distinguishes both signs and reads compensated material charge](../experiments/material_inertia/pair_spectrum/remote_charge/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. In a supplied uniformly filled medium a freely released excess and vacancy have zero relative load charge. A separated upper spectral band has an all-exposure lower bound of 85.47% for binding at the selected degree type on 20, 80 and 320 cells. At exposure 32 on 20 cells, 99.44% of terminal outcomes were adjacent, and in 6.83% both constituents had changed sites and were adjacent. Most motion stayed within the launch cell. An electromagnetic charge measurement and an independent inertial mass for this same excitation remain open. This test addresses OpenWave’s particle criterion. The OPH dark-sector paper assigns gravitational stress to anomalous modular charge on an overlap collar. Pair binding provides no measurement of that source. OpenWave lists OPH as untested on Row 19 at the cited revision. The official static test asks for a stable neutral massive state. It does not specify an elementary particle or a relic abundance. PATCH-10 tracks gravitational observations sourced by patch records. A separate coin-odd contrast-two memory has fixed-address native gaps of 32 or 33. Every one of the nine two-port load inputs acquires the same energy shift. Excess and vacancy Ramsey probes return bright with probability one at π/32, against zero for the matched even-coin contact. This identifies a stored mode invisible to these probes. Electromagnetic neutrality and a finite inertial mass require independent evidence. Matched collar correlations on a separate 20-cell native four-tracer instrument produce zero exact initial focusing contrast. At the later appointment their normalized chord volumes differ by −0.000005833 ± 0.000001756 at simultaneous 99% coverage. Testing anomalous gravitational stress requires a common proper curvature coupled to the collar, beyond this tracer geometry. This broader hypothesis is separate from the static particle criterion. Three free bound-pair preparations on a 20-cell uniformly filled host give measured intercell kinetic matrix elements near −1.02 to −1.04 × 10⁻⁶ exchange units. Their intact-pair terminal imbalance responds to the imposed phase. The branch polarizes under a spatially stepped signed-load impulse while its total relative load stays zero. Inertial mass from momentum dispersion and coupling to a physical electromagnetic field remain unmeasured. The lowest 24 lines of the free 20-cell bound band remain at least 99.36999% on adjacent high addresses. Three filtered sharp sources yield 6.8287%, 12.6035% and 18.8158% probability for an adjacent pair at least two owner steps from launch at one million exchange times. Time-zero probabilities are below 1.6 × 10⁻¹². The cluster has almost all its sharp-address weight on twelve cells of one gateway motif. A momentum-dispersion measurement across refinements and an independent electrical probe remain open. Independent opposite-sign contact probes on the same far-arriving pair resolve opposite spatial responses and a common scattering component of 0.219–0.224 single-charge response units at a 1/32 dwell. The whole-screen signed cancellation follows from the calibrated odd response and the equal constituent counts. On the 20-cell board a kinetic coefficient fixed at the first owner wavelength misses all six held peak forecasts on the moving band. A physical electrical response and a predictive inertial mass remain open. For the separate two-head collar proposal the connected preparation changes neither relative load nor the parked energy of 16. Its modular first variation has a calculable, reference-dependent sign. Physical mass and its gravitational effect need independent readings. Separately, a controlled anchored guide supports a conserved inverse-square energy profile at its scattering pole. On a selected finite head source, removing correlations at fixed ordinary exchange energy produces an additional measured area deficit of 0.0100028 at the ordinary survey coupling. Physical curvature, electrical neutrality, independent inertia and a galactic mass normalization remain open. In the lowest doublet of the same moving bound pair, a common impulse gives a positive band-selected centre recoil on all three tested launches. The immediate unfiltered response has the opposite sign. Physical mass and electromagnetic neutrality remain unestablished. For three initially selected lowest-doublet preparations on 20 cells, numerical enclosures of the terminal position mean include all post-impulse addresses. Doubling the common impulse and the flight duration gives a positive displacement within 10% of four times the calibration displacement in all three cases. These bounds use the native owner coordinate and exchange-time units. On three sharp launches filtered to the same 24-line moving bound cluster, the native charge census in each of the 20 cells distinguishes the joint charge distribution from a hard-core mixture with matching single-port marginals. After one million exchange units the excess and vacancy occupy different cells on 0.107% of accepted launches, against 29.33% to 50.54% in the mixture. After four further units with only exchange, this probability reaches 61.03% to 61.93%. The filter accepts about 13.27% of original starts. A Ramsey field interpretation requires a supplied charge coupling. Physical electromagnetic neutrality and an independent mass scale remain open. A neutral material dimer now probes the regional signed load of the same moving bound cluster through native refocused contacts. Six sharp calibration readings and 1,080 X-meter count banks give a simultaneous 99% upper bound of 0.1031% on uncompensated regional load, across 60 regions and three launches after one million exchange units. This bound is conditional on passing the spectral filter and the unit-filled meter-site test. At the cell of origin the three matched-marginal controls give lower bounds above 27%. The prepared dimer, addressed spatial profile and protected timing are supplied. The probe measures material load. Its connection to electromagnetism and an independently measured inertial mass remain open. A native parity and sign receiver gives every spectrally admitted pair a public signed-load reading, including launches that need a second or third meter site. Across 60 regions and three million-unit flights the simultaneous 99% upper count limit is 0.0992%. A separate numerical operator bound puts the probability of distinct owners below 0.107054% for every state in the 24-line cluster and all native free times. The summed energy response of a point-owner scalar Hamiltonian with arbitrary diagonal potential gives kinetic coefficient A in [2.0871,2.1519] × 10⁻⁶ on the calibration profile and [1.6845,1.7254] × 10⁻⁶ on a held profile of the same launch. A common scalar inertia fails the registered tolerance. These material, clock and shape measurements leave physical mass and electromagnetic charge unestablished. A receiver across a cell boundary reads the regional load of the same moving pair through a three-dimer polarizable guide. After two ticks, the sharp positive and negative standards each give 65,536 charged exits in 65,536 starts, and the host and compensated pair give zero. Cutting the bridge removes the response. Equality of the receiver effect and the original regional charge projector transfers the archived bound to this detector. These are material-load calibrations; physical electromagnetic neutrality and a common inertial mass remain open. - **Contrast memory** · **Demonstrated** · [Binding and local motion of a load-neutral pair](../experiments/material_inertia/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. This candidate and Interval Echo use the same native free generator in the filled sector. In a supplied uniformly filled medium a freely released excess and vacancy have zero relative load charge. A separated upper spectral band has an all-exposure lower bound of 85.47% for binding at the selected degree type on 20, 80 and 320 cells. At exposure 32 on 20 cells, 99.44% of terminal outcomes were adjacent, and in 6.83% both constituents had changed sites and were adjacent. Most motion stayed within the launch cell. An electromagnetic charge measurement and an independent inertial mass for this same excitation remain open. This test addresses OpenWave’s particle criterion. The OPH dark-sector paper assigns gravitational stress to anomalous modular charge on an overlap collar. Pair binding provides no measurement of that source. OpenWave lists OPH as untested on Row 19 at the cited revision. The official static test asks for a stable neutral massive state. It does not specify an elementary particle or a relic abundance. PATCH-10 tracks gravitational observations sourced by patch records. ### OW-20

**Electric force (Coulomb 1/r, electrostatics).** Measure an inverse-distance far-field potential sourced by one elementary electric charge. A strength claim also needs α derived from the mechanism.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L124](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L124)

- **Interval Echo · Open.** A constrained chain of native dimers transmits a static boundary interaction between independently signed material endpoints. On even-length guides its opposite-minus-like energy approaches 31.5 exchange units with an inverse-square correction. A constant plus inverse-distance forecast calibrated at four and six dimers misses all 19 held lengths on the 320-cell board. A pulse schedule calibrated from the dimer and contact energies produces an inverse-guide-length quasienergy magnitude. Two oppositely travelling material packets on the same critical guide move at a measured 0.955 wall spacings per exchange unit. The finite boundary phase and the packet speed are consistent with the scale of a critical one-dimensional mode. Keeping both charge signs fixed and adding one dimer reverses the signed interaction. A common electric potential depending only on physical distance and an electric strength remain unestablished. ### OW-21

**Lorentz covariance (+ Coulomb = EM).** Establish Lorentz covariance without a preferred inertial frame, including contraction of boosted states and an operational signal-speed bound.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L125](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L125)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [Motion-dependent shortening of a two-load state on a scheduled guide](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_21_boost/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. Guided finite-patch reunion counts track the calibrated time-dilation target on the tested service menu. A boost and a signal-speed limit on freely evolving spherical states remain open. Free contrast clusters retain the known ring inertia mismatch. In a spectrally selected bound pair on a supplied two-matching guide, the two endpoints contract with increasing centre speed. On 80 cells the measured length drops from 9.811 to 4.379 port spacings at 0.879 of the sharp single-load signal front. A midpoint fringe confirms the high-winding speed. The length ratio 0.446 misses the Lorentz forecast 0.477. Even allowing a free common speed misses the held widths on each board. These observations use the guide frame and its synchronized period-end appointments. On a separate 20-cell protocol, one addressed relay and a passive signal deliver different bit-decoding accuracies by the first or third local clock tick. Independently marked round trips also reach both windows. The two services have a supplied cadence. They establish finite message and clock records without defining a universal physical limiting speed or a boost law. On one 20-cell screen the addressed out-and-back service ranged three ports at two, four and six receiver ticks. A 52% decoding threshold chosen using the near pilot was fixed before testing the two farther ports. The near passive receiver decoded 137,471 of 262,144 bits by tick one. The middle decoded 132,275 at its forecast tick two and the held-out far port decoded 131,072 at tick three. Neither farther port qualified at any tested deadline through its forecast. Quiet outcomes and timeouts count. The addressed range specifies a service-dependent distance; passive ranging had zero marked returns at both farther ports. A stricter stationary hand requires the double at port 2 on every successive tick. The message and both echo legs use the same passive two-load flight. On the held-out sender, 57 and zero marked returns per 2³⁰ starts at six ticks forecast 50.00371–50.01474% one-way bit accuracy at three ticks. The independent test gave 50.01152%. The simultaneous 99% upper bound on the best classical reading of the full single-launch records is below 51.856% across the twelve native settings. The exposed practical threshold was 52%. The result identifies a reciprocal probability law under this clock read and supplied interrogation cadence. This single stationary clock does not establish a boost law or common physical speed. On the commissioned 49-dimer strand the fastest of seven measured packet speeds was 0.9903, against an exact maximum group speed of one and a long-wavelength dispersion coefficient c of four. The two smallest measured wave numbers give k/v of 0.948 and 0.954, compared with the inverse band curvature 15/16. At k=0.800 an independent energy read separates the initial bare-wall velocity from its accrued motion. Comparison with another signal is required to identify a common spacetime speed. ### OW-22

**Strong force: confinement.** Measure a linear confining potential between identified colour charges and recover a string tension near 1 GeV/fm.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L126](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L126)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [Constrained string energy and screening from two face-parity records](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_22_confinement/parity_sources/README.md). Full criterion · Open. On the 20-, 80- and 320-cell federations a locally decoded triplet and antitriplet give a colour-dependent contact. On the largest board the selected octet contact is −0.176961 ± 0.000149 and the singlet contact −0.000015 ± 0.000049 exchange units. At separations of two through 22 owner steps the connected operator is zero on all nine allowed fixed-source colours. A separate domain in a prepared polarized medium costs 17 units per displaced cell. The same outer wall records also occur at cost 34 for every tested length of at least two when extra interior walls are admitted. With a fourth load freely moving on 924 exterior ports, the 80-cell singlet gains 0.10937 exchange units at two steps relative to its best isolated-source binding, 0.00305 at four and less than 0.000001 at ten. This finite-range attraction saturates on the tested placements. A colour-selected separation energy and a physical string tension require an independent measurement. The stationary determinant of three native compact orbitals has exactly zero connected energy on the nearest eligible owners and at pair distances 15, 15 and 16 on the 320-cell board. Its material has zero amplitude on the gateways. On a second fixed period-three string the measured rung contribution changes by −17 native units per translated material unit. Translating the whole ring removes the endpoints while retaining the full contribution. This measures a bulk bias in the supplied reference. Above any blank homogeneous host, the full native energy of an arbitrary state is at most 198 times its expected number of material deviations plus 33 times its expected number of written seams. Unbounded linear growth requires an increasing number of excitations. At fixed total load on the active exterior bridges, local mismatch records around each face give an exact static energy of 32d when precisely two faces are odd. Holding only the same two odd readings allows screening and gives 32 min(d,2). At eleven face steps the work meter gives 352.005 and 64.002 native energy units. Opening an interior native contact can create the additional odd pair. The face-parity source, exterior contact schedule and face-step ruler are specified. Independently identifying colour, distance and the physical tension remains open. Two bounded additive local sources on a common state family have connected ground work at most the smaller spectral width. For endpoint families related by transposing two whole owners and their internal records, the absolute relaxed energy shift is at most 390 at arbitrary occupancy and separation. On 20, 80 and 320 owners independent whole-board and boundary spectra agree in 18 native and exchange-only comparisons, including a positive private-port control. A nonlocal flux constraint can evade the relocation hypothesis. For the all-open, half-filled binary blank sector with private port services included, imposing just two odd-face readings costs less than 19,129 native units above the free ground, uniformly in separation. This bound admits a finite linear window. ### OW-23

**Running coupling.** Resolve a coupling that changes with measurement scale, including the onset near the physical core radius.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L127](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L127)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [Timed collision readout distinguishes two-body interactions on a prepared guide](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_23_coupling/timed_scattering/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. Scattering from one empty seam write measures a two-port effective interaction at two spatial cutoffs on 20 and 80 cells. Using the coarse interaction at the finer cutoff misses a held third-port return by 0.09734 and 0.09738. Matching the interaction at the finer cutoff predicts both returns within 0.000004. An independent bound-load measurement gives a core radius of 0.538 links. Halving the spectrometer linewidth moves the first ten-percent change of the scattering amplitude from kR about 0.89 to 0.30 or 0.33. At fixed probe energy the first resolved ten-percent change of the cutoff-matched interaction is bracketed by kR = 0.113–0.184 and 0.124–0.135 on 20 and 80 cells, at both spectrometer widths. Fresh held receiver counts resolve the predicted change. This lies below the specified core window [0.5,2]. Its identification as a gauge coupling requires a further physical measurement. The written impurity, cutoff and instrument are supplied. A separate 80-cell addressed probe tests a freely moving two-load core on a guided ring. An independent radius measurement gives 6.588 port spacings at rest and 2.376 in motion. A 10% drop in apparent scattering amplitude shifts from momentum bin 3 to bin 7. The measured core shape with one fixed contact strength predicts all 213 nonforward bins of each preparation. Repeated scattering from adjacent loads predicts the two-contact signal. The onset intervals for momentum times radius straddle 0.5. These records identify shape and multiple contacts as sources of scale dependence on this guide. On a timed two-double guide the collision response is exactly blind to the flight contrast after one matching period. After sixteen, the two source preparations distinguish the interaction in 107 and 106 of 107 positive momentum bins. For the narrow source the first certified 10 percent crossing moves from bin 65 at four periods to bin 4 at sixteen, with initial-radius qR between 0.196 and 0.296 at sixteen. On a separate written seam, a single endpoint shift of −24.00003 ± 0.00299 predicts 24 held native remote readings for a second tag on 20 and 80 cells. Copying the first tag fails in 20. These results identify a timed material interaction and local tag response. A physical gauge coupling and core-set onset remain open. On the ordinary blank unit-filled host, the connected static energy of two nonadjacent ideally pinned material sources vanishes on every finite board. The charge-product component reads −16.25 ± 0.0625 on a direct seam. The same three-link detector resolves +0.453125 ± 0.0625 when a comparison interaction allows creation of a neutral dipole. A mobile mediator gives a nonzero connected return between remote neutral dipoles. With equal sharp static source energies, source reversal changes the thermal sign. Averaging orientations restores attraction at four noncontact separations, but the three successive inferred inverse-radius strengths have disjoint ranges. In the published candidate an empty-seam measurement forecasts rest and all five moving frequencies from the exact four-site dispersion within 0.036%. The later eight-witness common-unit standard forecasts those same archived frequencies within 0.032%, with its scale fixed by the comparison rule. Fair parity gives about 50% discrepancies. The forecasts share the prepared one-dimensional material background. This single-load gap depends on the prepared route. Shortest internal paths joining the same gateway port pairs through the same 194 cells put 62 modes in the original gap. Locally commissioned empty repairs split a single-load spectrum into coordination bands on 20, 80 and 320 cells in two independent preparations per size. In the selected one-write band, all 30 constant and low spatial-mode preparations fail the single-frequency test. The strongest two adjacent clock bins hold 34.23–50.07% of the tagged zero-mode counts, below the 80% pure-line floor after leakage accounting. A uniform preparation restricted to connected one-write ports passes the frequency filter on over 97% of launches. The joint frequency and spatial return counts still reject a single rest frequency on all six media at simultaneous 99% confidence. Free transport depends strongly on the deposited records. On 27 independently prepared local sharp-repair histories, a uniform wave gave 36.48–59.11% home misses at one quarter exchange unit, with zero on exchange-only material. The squared frequency width is κ² times the spatial variance of the incident write count on that wave. A separate frequency-gated detector four cells from the source gave 2,220 of 4,194,304 mid-gap arrivals on the shortest path and zero anywhere on the repeated path. On the commissioned 49-dimer strand the fastest of seven measured packet speeds was 0.9903, against an exact maximum group speed of one and a long-wavelength dispersion coefficient c of four. The two smallest measured wave numbers give k/v of 0.948 and 0.954, compared with the inverse band curvature 15/16. At k=0.800 an independent energy read separates the initial bare-wall velocity from its accrued motion. At a native three-arm dimer junction the two outgoing cells jointly recorded walls on 10,470,504 of 268,435,456 incident-wall starts versus 71,370 on the zero-wall control. A scalar continuation of the calibrated strand predicts an exclusive turn probability of at least 0.000615; all eight held settings had at most one detection per 268,435,456 starts. Four straight-arm controls agreed. The single-wall graph continuation fails at this fork. The one-dimensional dispersion result keeps its stated domain. A 14-dimer, three-cell strand yields a signed impulse response at 15 positions and 61 times from even two-time products. The largest displacement error against the independently calibrated discrete wave operator is 0.001513; matched hopping misses by 0.21674 against its 0.07548 allowance. On the two measured long-wave modes the ordinary continuum comparison differs by 0.001701 within a separate 0.03963 allowance. The lattice result concerns the prepared strand, and public state/decoder admission and a prospective upstream acquisition remain outstanding. ### OW-31

**Orbital quantization (atomic structure).** Observe standing-wave bound levels with a hydrogen-like spectrum, including characteristic spacings.

Criterion · [MODELS.md L137](https://github.com/openwave-labs/openwave/blob/d4fc3e4046c472f4ad38468df8dddfa1f97bac97/MODELS.md#L137)

- **Interval Echo** · **Demonstrated** · [Local orbital spectroscopy and a public read of core erasure](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_31_orbitals/native_core_release/README.md). Full criterion · Partial. A load bound to a static seam has discrete levels in the free contrast interaction. The computed spacings and shell multiplicities fail the hydrogenic comparison, and the level count at fixed record number is bounded under refinement. A freely moving amount-one and amount-two pair gives 1,032,974 adjacent detections among 1,034,018 bound-window clicks on the 20-cell board. An isolated cell has a relative standing-wave level at −30.1213. On 20, 80 and 320 cells all negative levels of the mixed pair lie in [−32,−24] relative to the separated reference. The second and third hydrogenic levels would fall in the empty spectral gap. The analogous test also excludes these two levels for each equal-amount pair on the empty host. A separate mobile load mediates attraction between two stationary written cores. Calibrating an inverse-distance law at three links predicts 0.05415 exchange units at four; the gains are 0.001893 and 0.000204 on 20 and 80 cells. All 18 held distance settings miss that prediction. Other media and longer-range interactions are outside these experiments. With a single stationary written seam and a mobile load, exactly two levels lie below the blank dissociation reference on the 20-, 80- and 320-cell boards. An endpoint phase pulse resolves their transition at 0.47994 and 0.47993 exchange units on the two smaller boards. The measured ground binding predicts a hydrogenic transition of 4.6021, which would place the final energy near −1.534 in the empty spectral interval. This rules out that spectrum for this supplied core and preparation. A native vacuum echo prepares the stationary core. A local sharp load and a calibrated finite energy tag prepare its ground orbital with about 96% admission. On 20 and 80 cells the endpoint pulse resolves the 0.47993 transition and gives zero clicks at the 4.602 hydrogenic forecast in 4,194,304 launches per board. A native π contact converts blank versus vacuum-written core memory into a public terminal load position. After one attempted erase and 64 units of flight this gives 74,874 of 4,028,341 and 74,983 of 4,029,302 admitted blank-core readings. For one mobile load and any stationary pattern of empty writes, the negative index equals the number of touched ports. Relative to the blank-host reference, the full spectra exclude (−3,0) for amount one and (−27,0) for amount two. A hydrogenic accumulation at that threshold is impossible on this family across refinements. Mobile cores and other matter sectors lie outside the bound. For any two disjoint stationary empty-written cores the ground attraction mediated by a single unit load has an upper bound proportional to (6/17)^d in the separation d of the nearest written ports, uniformly in board size and core shape. At two held separations on 320 cells both core shapes returned zero dark exits in 1,048,576 launches each, against independently calibrated inverse-distance forecasts above 99.88%. ## Hypothetical gravitational particle

Track a quantum of the gravitational field alongside the Standard Model inventory. A classical spin-two wave and a quantum excitation receive distinct tests.

### GR-graviton

**Graviton.** Tie a prepared or emitted neutral excitation to two transverse tidal polarizations on independent observers, a massless spin-two propagation law, universal coupling to stress and a positive quantum one-particle response on the same construction.

Criterion · [deriving_standard_model_gauge_structure_from_observer_overlap_consistency.tex](../../paper/deriving_standard_model_gauge_structure_from_observer_overlap_consistency.tex) · [observer_patch_holography_as_string_vacuum_selector.tex](../../paper/archive/observer_patch_holography_as_string_vacuum_selector.tex) · [VacuumTidalRead.lean](../../Lean/Geometry/VacuumTidalRead.lean)

- **Interval Echo · Open.** On 20 native cells, a material receiver gives identical histories for the tested equal-energy quadrupole settings until ordinary material contact is restored. A remote contact signal can distinguish the orientations. Independent differential arm records tied to propagated curvature and a quantum-gravity response remain open. The registered one-load spectrum has a five-dimensional orbital quadrupole at a gap of three native units relative to the uniform one-load state. A bright combination crosses a boundary, while two or three independent combinations per cell remain trapped on each tested board. In the free one-load sector the normalized local orbital quadrupole weight is at most one third of the energy above the uniform reference. Face-wide releases give sampled peak weights of 1.10%, 0.66% and 0.34% across the three resolutions. A material orbital measurement has restricted sensitivity to low-energy modes. ## Standard Model particle identifications

Identify all 17 elementary particle types on one frozen candidate. Each species has a local preparation or production channel, reproducible detection statistics, and a dictionary that distinguishes its nearest alternatives. A declaration of a field or an input mass cannot identify the excitation.

### SM-u

**Up.** Recover the spin-half colour-triplet sector, its electric and weak responses, and a flavour discriminator in colour-singlet production, decay or scattering records. Resolve the conjugate sector. Light up-type flavour, charge +2/3.

Criterion · [contents_listings.html](https://pdg.lbl.gov/2026/listings/contents_listings.html) · [deriving_the_particle_zoo_from_observer_consistency.tex](../../paper/deriving_the_particle_zoo_from_observer_consistency.tex) · [PROOF.md](../../code/defect_particle_classes/PROOF.md) · [physical_identification_registry.json](../../claims/physical_identification_registry.json)

- **Interval Echo · Open.** A static signed-field receiver measures the positive wall at +0.66640 ordinary units. Four-hop material records exclude an independent exact colour triplet in the tested intact written-cell component. A remote polarization guide also gives +0.6656 on a parked three-wall preparation. The exact compact orbital triplet carries a whole material unit. Electric charge and light up flavour are unassigned. ### SM-e

**Electron.** Resolve spin half, unit negative charge, colour neutrality and weak interactions. Discriminate the three generations and their positive-charge conjugates through spectral or decay measurements. Light stable charged-lepton branch.

Criterion · [contents_listings.html](https://pdg.lbl.gov/2026/listings/contents_listings.html) · [deriving_the_particle_zoo_from_observer_consistency.tex](../../paper/deriving_the_particle_zoo_from_observer_consistency.tex) · [PROOF.md](../../code/defect_particle_classes/PROOF.md) · [physical_identification_registry.json](../../claims/physical_identification_registry.json)

- **Interval Echo · Open.** The freely released bare pair responds to the centre of the tested local SU(3) current. Its terminal records also distinguish all five alternative port-label permutations. On a unit-filled background an independently prepared signed defect transports two coherent orbital modes through two cell boundaries. A controlled full turn and opposite orders of perpendicular half-turns give the ordinary positive relative phase. Filtering the local colour band at both endpoints retains coherent transfer, but the leading arrival from a localized launch has one quarter of its weight in that band. The compact 35-unit vacancy can release charge after a phase pulse, but its one-vacancy spectrum excludes the anchored relativistic recoil. A repulsively bound two-vacancy and one-excess cluster rearranges inside its home cell. Its upper-band energy above the uniform host also fails this recoil test. A physical colour-singlet assignment, signed electromagnetic response, spatial spin half and an electron–positron identification require further evidence on the same excitation. [README.md](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_04_identity/README.md) · [README.md](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_02_dressed/README.md) · [README.md](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_04_conjugates/README.md) · [README.md](../experiments/interval_echo_angular/charged_orientation/README.md) · [README.md](../experiments/interval_echo_gauge/singlet_flight/README.md)
  - **Written-field Laplacian extension** · An independent boundary-flux read tracks the mobile signed load, and a probe detects the confined source 22 cells away. This makes the load a strong candidate for the electron’s charge and field response. Its three-input counts exclude spin-½ fermions with matched spin-independent transfers. A four-face orbital doublet can transfer coherently with the charge. During an addressed material exchange the two different orbital states stay in their original cells. In the complete finite one-cell sectors tested, all 5,790 vacancy and 1,954 excess energies are simple. Two vacancies with resolved field endpoints give either exchange sign under different intermediate reads of the same source. A freely transported spin doublet and an independently calibrated rest energy in keV are unresolved. A separate first-order mass construction realizes the geometric family ratios on a guided seven-cell charge, with strong dependence on the guide and a supplied register mass operator. Each rest level on the undressed positive guide has one state per charge sign. A monitored two-face vacancy has localized negative-level partners. One unlabeled source resolves three frequencies. Their middle ratio exceeds the muon ratio by 9.83 ppm. Scalar forces on the charge position preserve the parked pair. Positive recoil frequencies on the moving guide independently recover the three dispersion parameters from port counts. Local source selection and distal charge reads join localization and finite-wavelength inertia on this guide. A spinless orbital realization gives identical charge and flux histories. [Comparison study](../candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/electron_field/README.md)

The transverse field, quantization, clock and charged path operations belong to this comparison. ## Agreement across theorems and experiments

### JOINT-one-law

Every premise assigned to the law is fulfilled by one unchanged law on one process.

- **Interval Echo · Open.** No joint realization is registered.
- **Contrast memory · Open.** No joint realization is registered.
- **Private record walk · Open.** No joint realization is registered.
- **Scalar channel · Open.** No joint realization is registered.

### JOINT-einstein-together

The stress that observers infer from modular flow is the stress carried by the excitations that propagate and interact under the law.

- **Interval Echo · Partial.** An unprotected mobile load correlates independent armed seams. A fresh probe detects the correlation at matched local states. The sharp modular reference measures shared information. The native free generator obeys additive patch energy continuity. On the earlier pair, a prepared cap reads weighted seam energy at a held port where the flat reference failed. An interior exchange changes this cap's entropy with its complete gateway state fixed. Time-reversal-odd one-load seam currents exhibit torque. Load probes are exactly blind to an antisymmetric collar's stored energy. A parent Hamiltonian predicts held modular changes on nested patches. Two interior modes with identical exterior single-load histories yield different entropy slopes. On a matched free-flight calibration the same source deflects the unit tag and leaves the double tag transparent. Both species detect a supplied routing change. Free evolution conserves the routing flags. Shared and fresh collar preparations agree in initial four-tracer focusing while their later chord volumes differ. A prepared ancestor-tagged collar on shrinking clocked annuli transfers a fourth-power charge coefficient with zero cross charge between far annuli. Nearby separated cuts retain joint charge. The protected routes bypass the source. Physical area, a conserved directional stress and the common curvature response to matter remain open. ### JOINT-matter-together

Gauge currents act on those excitations through the law's operations; detector, generator, clock and currents refer to the same object.

- **Interval Echo · Partial.** A compiled signed current pulse acts on the bound pair used for free flight and seam detection. The source and detector are calibrated on supplied windows. Timing that flight by a hand on the same object and establishing complete physical gauge currents remain open. ### JOINT-patch-measurement

Finite patches measure both the current response and the stress of one bound excitation with their own clocks and records.

- **Interval Echo · Partial.** The earlier four-cell finite meter reads the thermal-reference modular generator of the current-pulsed pair using 4,423 settings with exact probabilities. Its 31-setting sampled meter did not resolve the during-flight contrast. The exposure is externally timed and a directional spacetime stress observable remains open. ### JOINT-relativistic-matter

The rest energy and the inertia of every bound excitation follow one dictionary.

- **Interval Echo · Counterexample.** At kappa = 8 the kinetic ratios of clusters of two, three and four loads (about 34, 1,155 and 39,236) differ from their rest-energy ratios (about 1.686, 2.354 and 3.020); a common relativistic mass would make them equal. [README.md](../experiments/material_inertia/README.md)
